The first dates, both bilaterally and in larger working groups with the national councils and councillors, are already agreed for autumn. We start this process in order to approach the best possible way for the second free kindergarten year – in any form it will always be realized. ParlaMint-AT_2016-07-07-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00138_d7e7487	Madam President. I am not only glad that we have been adopting the health and nursing law discussed for months or better for years with all the stakeholders, but I am also glad that the plant is working again, as it is a very important law that we will decide today. Honestly, at the beginning of last week – because I see Mr Rasinger sitting here – I have not personally been able to believe that we are making this decision today; but I say: Good thing needs a while. I believe that we have been able to reach an acceptable agreement for all involved, bringing about significant changes in the care of patients. What does that mean? . The present draft provides for the introduction of a three-tier training system, as my previous speaker said, in future. In other words, the previous care assistance will now be upgraded to care assistance, while one year's training remains, but the job profile will be updated and adapted to the actual needs, because in the theoretical part of this training – and this is important in my opinion – a focus will also be on long-term care. Especially at a time when people are not least getting older due to good medical care and therefore the care effort will increase dramatically over the next few years or decades, in my view, this is a more than necessary step. Another innovation in this law is the introduction of a nursing professional assistance, which can continue to be trained at the health and nursing schools as well as the care assistance. This new professional image, which is basically intended once for initial vocational training, will further relieve the elevated service because the care professional will be transferred more skills in the future. Yes, and how does the elevated service finally look like? – There you have to look at the countries around Austria: There it is already going on everywhere and there would be that the education for the elevated service in the health and health care sector is located in the university area. [[Abg. Rasinger: Except in Germany!]] – With the exception of Germany, I'll give you the right. But so far, I think we have had a typical Austrian solution: One was trained in the nursing school, the other in the field of vocational colleges. This training will now be raised to the level of the technical colleges. I believe it is important that this parallelity, this parallelity, is finally put to an end. We have a transitional period for organizational reasons up to 2024, and then all are trained at high school level. And what I think is very important is the permeability within these three professions. That means I can actually work from the care assistance to the elevated service. I am convinced that with this law we have taken a path in the right direction. On the one hand, it continues to ensure high quality in training, and on the other hand, the distribution of tasks at the workplace will improve the care and care of patients. Since many concerns have also been expressed – we have received countless e-mails every day; because of this new legislative proposal, the federal states have also promoted savings potential, and many have asked if there are any improvements in quality at all – we have requested an evaluation for a year and a half, in which all experts from the Ministry of Health, but also from the countries and the interest groups will participate. It will be seen that there is no deterioration in quality. Those who have bombarded us with e-mails in the last few days, I might want to give one along the way: With structures of the fifties of the last century, we cannot have a health policy of the 21st century. ==References== Charles Darwin once said: “It’s not the strongest species that survives, nor the most intelligent, it’s the one that can best adapt to change.”

At that time, the disputes arose about who was allowed to do something in the hospital bed, and among them the working climate of all suffered – both the doctors and the health and health care professionals – and the patients were also suffering, because of the fact that they often have to wait until the two agreed who can do something in the hospital bed or with the sick person. One of the topics that we have tried to redefine and regulate in this law – and I believe that this has really been successful – is to clarify by law and not by dispute on the bed, who is responsible for what. Then, of course, we have also tried to create career opportunities for women – health and health care is still a women's profession – which are permeable, from one-year training to care assistance, to two-year training to nursing professional assistance to tertiary training. Mrs. Col. Refuserstorfer has presented the above-mentioned mail to the works councils, in which it is raised that people working on human beings are less trained as animal feeders. Quite honestly, I have always been a staff representative and I am a trade unionist with both body and soul, but in this letter, the works councils say that the people who have been working side by side with them since I started studying and even since I started studying and still earlier, after a one-year training at the hospital bed, would be less trained as an animal care officer, and I find this to be very inappropriate as a staff representative. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Animal care is a teaching profession, that has always been the case, and health care is not a teaching profession, but a modular training. The countries have blocked this law for a very long time because they have said it will cost them too much. The trade unions have said that the law is too cheap. So I think we have found a good compromise. I'm proud of this law, but we need to keep looking at it. ParlaMint-AT_2016-07-07-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00138_d7e8798	The next speaker was Mr Keck. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2016-07-07-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00138_d7e8820	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Austria complies with a requirement of the EU Services Directive with the amendment of the Veterinary Act, colleague Refuserstorfer. In the context of the evaluation of the implementation of this Directive, the European Commission pointed out that, in its view, the minimum tariffs provided for in professional veterinary law, as currently provided for in the Austrian Veterinary Chamber’s fee system, constitute a restriction on freedom to provide services. That's why we're changing this law here. In discussions with representatives of the European Commission, it became clear that the mandatory minimum tariffs in accordance with veterinary professional law, as provided for in the fees regulations, should be abolished, otherwise infringement proceedings against Austria will be initiated. This would result in Austria being most likely to be condemned for breach of these obligations, with all the financial and legal consequences attached to them. The fee scheme should be converted to allow the Austrian Veterinary Board to lay down its members' rules on accounting principles and to submit guidelines for veterinary services in the form of a recommendation, but no longer to impose a minimum requirement. Since the amount of fees is no longer a legally binding requirement, the consultation and approval procedure is also void. This was necessary if it was legally binding, but that is no longer the reason for it, and not because the EU wants to do so. The Austrian Veterinary Chamber therefore has more room for manoeuvre in the design of the fee system. However, the Federal Ministry of Health, as the supervisory authority, continues to do away with illegal contents of the fee system. Mein Ladies and gentlemen, this does not only concern the Chamber of Veterinars with regard to the Services Directive, but also concerns, for example, architects, lawyers and so on; it must also be amended in the relevant legislation on the matter.

It is precisely in this area that the European Union has criticised the conditions in Austria and even threatened a infringement procedure, because the fees of the veterinarians in Austria have so far required approval by the Federal Minister for Health and Women and because the approval was granted only after consultation of the Chamber of Commerce, the Austrian Chamber of Workers, the Austrian Trade Union Confederation and the Presidential Conference of the Agriculture Chambers of Austria. Now we have the same thing in dark green. Fortunately, this authorisation procedure under the Veterinary Act had to be deleted, and the veterinarians and their office representatives gave themselves a fee order. The whole thing just happened without political pride and without the involvement of politics. In our opinion, this example should be a school, and the social partners actually belong to all the topics that concern free groups of professionals themselves. There is therefore no agreement from our side, because we believe that this cannot be a climate in which a free economy in Austria can thrive.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-07-07-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00138_d7e11330	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Annotated infectious disease Today, in Austria, God is very rare. This is also a sign of quality in the Austrian health system. However, in order for these figures to continue to fall in the future so that they can also be optimally addressed to new developments, the Tuberculosis Act and the Epidemic Law are to be amended today. The reporting obligation for infectious diseases has been established in Austria to prevent the spread of serious infectious diseases. This is not only about the medical treatment of the persons concerned, but above all about the investigation of the environment, environmental analyses, but also the investigation and persecution of the infection chain up to the sources of diseases and, of course, also the information of vulnerable contact persons. Additional cases of disease are included in the Epidemic Act today. The Chikungunya and Dengue fever, but also the Zika and Hanta virus are new to the Epidemic Act. Dealing with tuberculosis cases is regulated in Austria in its own law, in the Tuberculosis Act. There are the core areas of 1968. That is why it is important that this law has also been repealed with the involvement of experts and with the involvement of specialist companies. On the one hand, this is about extending the scope of application, i.e. also diseases in people caused by various tuberculosis pathogens. There is also an extension of the treatment requirement for people suffering from tuberculosis, which is not contagious but should be treated until final healing. It is also a duty to inform new medical possibilities for people at risk of infection who were in contact. I also believe that there will be a clear definition of the tasks of the Reference Centre, where an annual report on tuberculosis cases will be drawn up. It was, of course, established that the necessary data base must at least be available to the reference centre. It was also important that, in the event that people refuse treatment, we have re-regulated the issue of the restriction of freedom in the event of inaccurate tuberculosis, which is also based on constitutional and human rights. To sum up, I think that with this proposal for a law today, we are making a good and important further development for Austria with the aim of significantly reducing the frequency of infectious diseases in the future.

Like my previous speaker, I also have to start my contribution with the lunch break paid. Mr Loacker, you have presented this as if the public service in relation to working time were inadequately preferred to the private sector. But look at reality! The public service has a weekly working time of 40 hours. Most collective agreements provide for a weekly working time of 38.5 hours for the private sector. You will also find collective agreements, which also provide for a paid lunch break in the private sector. I can therefore ask you to show more fairness to the officials and not to operate civil servants' bashing. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] Official bashing is of course popular, but I ask for fairness and objectivity! – This is not a client policy, but a fair presentation of the facts, and the facts must be taken into account, please. [[Call:This is cheap client policy what you're doing]] My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen, the present Novelle Service Law is a successful package which contains a number of important individual measures. It should be stressed that in the future more attention will be paid to the fact that there can be more and more stages in professional life in which one is unable to achieve 100%. Accordingly, account is taken of the fact that even exceptional events in the service context can lead to mental stress disturbances that temporarily do not permit the provision of services. Such periods will in future be irrelevant for the calculation of the period from when a flat-rate ancillary fee rests. Furthermore, it is foreseen that the existing requirement of full capacity for action may be assumed in each case if there is the capacity to act for the intended use. In order to facilitate or facilitate the return to service after a longer period of illness, judges are given the opportunity to reduce their burden. This also applies to incurably ill. Another request, which was already very important to me as Minister of Justice, is now also being implemented. This is about the possibility of using explosive judges to bridge the time of the MM employment ban. This allows for a rapid and flexible response to musculoskeletal absences, particularly at smaller services. I have been greatly disturbed by the previous regulation that the high rate of women has been detrimental to justice. This will finally be remedied. As we already know from other service law novels, there are also European adjustments, in particular to the implementation of ECJ knowledge. For example, the improvement in the calculation of the holiday replacement benefit due to the ECJ ruling Schultz-Hoff is mentioned. Finally, private labour law also serves as a basis for some change in the law of employment. For example, the agreement of competition clauses will only be allowed in the future if the monthly fee exceeds 20 times the daily ASVG maximum contribution basis. For 2016, this will result in a fee limit of €3 240. There are also improvements in the reimbursement of training costs. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen, although these individual measures are to be welcomed, they must not let us forget that the government programme provides for the modernisation of the law of the service. I therefore call on you, Mrs State Secretary, to start the social partnership negotiations with the Public Service Union as soon as possible, as provided for in the Coalition Agreement. Thank you.

For example, by offering more than 53 teaching occupations, the federal government is the most important and largest apprentice trainer in Austria. This shows that we are making a progressive policy, but it also shows that the diversity we live is re-imagining in the public service. Ladies and gentlemen! These are the priorities I would like to set. I would like to promote equality between women and men in the public service. I would like to strengthen the diversity of our society in the public service. [[Abg. Stefan: What does that mean?]] We want people to be able to bring their potentials and skills to public service as best they can, and to strengthen diversity in their composition, with different ways of life, multilingualism, different competences, public service. We already have so many and as well-trained, motivated employees in the public service, who highlight the joint work. In the public service, cooperation is so important for common objectives. I always say that the quality of the state is also due to the quality of its employees. To bring it to the point even more beautiful and better: The people who work in the public administration here stand for something very special, because they are the business card of the state. They represent the state and therefore have great responsibility. I cannot therefore often stress enough how much I was allowed to experience as a State Secretary in the public service in the commitment and professionalism of the staff. I would therefore like to express my sincere appreciation. But, ladies and gentlemen, at this point, it is my personal concern to also address personal words to the colleagues of the police who were in the police in Penzing last week, as the Secretary of State for the Public Service. The events and, above all, the consequences of this tragic incident on my colleagues have deeply affected me. I would therefore like to express my deep sympathy, my deep participation, my deep condolences to the colleagues of the police, especially the family and the relatives of the injured and the unfortunately deceased. [[General condolences.]] We have thus had to learn tragically what a risk lies in the high level of commitment of officials, namely, their daily lives for security, for the order of our society, but also for the fair and peaceful living together. I am in mind with my colleagues. Ladies and gentlemen! I can now briefly present the key points of the Novelle Service Law. It has already been said a lot, but there are some points that seem very important to me and which I would like to stress here again. On the one hand, we are concerned that we have for the first time included the acute mental burden response as a cause for prevention of service in the law. This means that we continue to pay compensation and flatrates during this period of service. So far, these regulations have only been valid because of physical prevention. For the first time, staff should not be too short due to special occupational stress situations, namely in exceptional events – we think of the use of fire disasters, in the recovery of bodies. With this new regulation, we also show how important the health of employees in the public service is and that we do not leave them alone in difficult situations. Especially among police officers, it is often the case that very extraordinary events in the exercise of their service can lead to mental stress reactions that make the mistake of the service impossible. With this new law, we want to close a gap here. Another point that I would like to mention here is that we have come closer to our goal of creating a single judge. The change of judges between administrative jurisdiction and ordinary jurisdiction is made possible. We thus increase the permeability between the courts, and we strengthen judicial professional mobility. The reform of the administrative jurisdiction, which took place on 1 January 1989. The entry into force of January 2014 will be taken into account once again. In connection with judges and judges, I should also like to point out that there is now also the possibility of part-time work on re-entry after a longer illness. That's good! As the last point, I can say that in this novel, which has already been mentioned, we are facilitating and clarifying the inclusion of people with disabilities. We have changed the legislative text in such a way that we no longer speak of the full ability to act, but of the necessary capacity to act. This will also clarify the inclusion of people with disabilities in the public service for the future. This has also been a special concern to me as a specialist in the field of diversity. Finally, I would like to address a point that has been put forward in the Constitutional Committee by the Green Members, namely the point in the new law, which is to extend the rights of witnesses in the field of disciplinary law. For the first time, victims of sexual harassment can contribute to a trustee in the proceedings before the Disciplinary Commission. The Green Members' proposal was to go beyond that and also to provide for counter-dictive hearings, as we know them in the criminal procedure order, in disciplinary law. I have taken this from the last Committee on Constitutional Affairs, and we have also considered this matter in detail. There is already the possibility of audiovisual hearing in disciplinary proceedings in the Civil Service Service Law for minors in the public service. My proposal would be to take this point with me during the next Service Law Negotiation and to extend this audiovisual experience to adults in future. Here I will take advantage of this opportunity and discuss it with the Public Service Trade Union as a social partners. We thus show that the protection of employees in the public service is also so important to us. Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen! I think that this service law novel brings significant improvements to employees in the public service. I wish I could and I hope you will agree. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-07-07-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00138_d7e17463	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen! Unfortunately, in the previous year there were over 4,000 road accidents involving moped or motorcyclists throughout Austria, and unfortunately 90 two-wheelers were killed. If you take a closer look at the statistics, you realize that there are certain risk groups for accidents, especially with motorcycles: first men in general – the probability that a man is injured or killed in a motorcycle accident is almost seven times higher than in a woman – and second young men under 20 and men between 40 and 50. – These two groups are mainly involved in accidents. Driving practice and the critical review of the respective skills – I repeat it – must be the A and O of every driving licence training. And precisely to ensure this repeated critical review of the respective driving skills and practice, the so-called multi-phase training was launched in 2003. To remember: In the case of a Class A driver's licence, the law provides that a driver's safety training must be completed in the period from two to twelve months after the existing test and that a so-called perfection journey is to be carried out in the period from four to 14 months after a driver's licence test. Up to now, these perfection trips were only to be stopped by driving schools and not by the already addressed automobile clubs ARBÖ or ÖAMTC. Ladies and gentlemen, with the novel of the driver's license law to be decided today, the rides of perfection as well as the training in the context of stage access for classes A, A1 and A2 are also released for car drivers' clubs. The much larger circle of suppliers will hopefully – and I really hope so – have a positive impact on competition and, of course, on the price. At the same time, very much revered Ladies and gentlemen, it is of course important to ensure the high quality of training. It is therefore clear that it is positive to stress that it is precisely defined which qualifications the trainees need and that there is also a comprehensive obligation to documentation. Mr. Willi, dear Georg, I really believe that by today's decision of the 17th. FSG-Novelle ensures a higher quality of driving training, which in turn is a big step towards more safety on Austrian roads. Our common goal should be that there are no more road fatalities on Austria's roads, and that is precisely a driving training with a lot of practice. No matter who does, it must be good people who train the motorcycles. And this is secured with the novel, ladies and gentlemen, to be decided today! Thank you.

Let's say it again. We don't let anyone drive independently who's watching anything on the TV by the way. It is about testing on specially selected roads, where the driver is primarily responsible for what is happening, and where there can be no situations where a machine has to decide whether it has to die – these are things that are not going on right now. ParlaMint-AT_2016-07-07-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00138_d7e19347	Mr President. Minister! The initiative proposal 1741/A is aimed at simplifying the authorisation of departures and landings outside of airfields. The current regulation states that, in principle, such departures and landings are allowed only with the approval of the respective head of state after carrying out a weighing of interests. In addition, the exact place of departure and arrival must also be announced in advance, and this is often not possible or limited only in case of emergency landings, rescue operations, disaster aid, hang and paragliders, or even sailing flights or balloon rides. In order to enable departures and landings outside of airfields to be carried out in accordance with the law, it is now necessary to establish that the external or external authorisation may be granted even without specifying the specific area in the form of a general authorisation. The condition is that conditions and/or conditions can ensure that public interests can be safeguarded. So this is really the prerequisite, unlike what you said, Mr Willi. The same applies to the practical execution of landings of parachute jumpers and free balloons. In the case of outdoor landings with parasols or free balloons outside of densely populated territory, no public interest is affected. The following is to be determined that no authorisation is required under § 9 for outdoor landings with parachutes and outdoor departures from free balloons outside densely populated areas.

I would also like to add that in this law we also regulate that the firing of fireworks within security zones of airports is permitted if the airport has no operating time. There are very many small airports that close at some time at 22 or 23 o'clock, and then the firing of fireworks is allowed in the future, but of course not within operating hours, because this would be a dangerous situation. We are convinced that this innovation is a sensible one, really in the sense of the people concerned, the air sportsmen, aviation and rescue services. As for your request, Mr. Georg Willi, regarding the Austro Control: That sounds great, of course. But you have forgotten to say that the Austro Control is actually allowed to keep only 2 percent profit. All other funds, which are taken in or have been budgeted in advance, must be reimbursed again within the framework of Single European Sky. Also of this €13 million profit – which sounds of course very beautiful – no €13 million remained, but €9 million were returned to the individual airlines. Therefore, the approach may be welcome, but simply not feasible. - Thank you.

Now there can be the interest, I want to drive faster – okay. There is also the interest of safety in transport, which I believe, at higher speeds. And then there is the interest of health, especially of children, of old people, but also of all of us. For me, it's clear what interest is just about. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] No one decrees an air hundred or eighty simply for fun, but we have been told what's behind it. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Wheeler.]] Firstly, we must apply the precautionary principle in order to avoid health hazards and early deaths. Secondly, there is also a right of citizens to a healthy environment, to clean air. Some citizens also try to fight this because individual countries are setting too few measures to protect the health of citizens. Thirdly, we simply have valid rights. There are infringement proceedings against Austria, not only as regards fine dust, but also as regards nitrogen oxides. So we need comprehensive action here. The competence lies in the air quality anyway at the federal level. The High Authority is the Minister for the Environment, who should also intervene if too few measures are put in place. Against this background, I do not think the question of competence is appropriate either. We will reject the request and continue to work to ensure that sufficient measures are taken to protect the health of the Austrian population. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-07-07-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00138_d7e22242	Mr President. Estimated Minister of State! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Our Austrian Patent Office, with its proud 117 years, is certainly one of the oldest legal institutions in the world. It has increased significantly in recent years – and this is what we are getting in international location and innovation competition – the role of intellectual property in recent years and will, of course, continue to gain significantly in importance through a very offensive patent law policy of many countries – China is an example for this. I believe that, not only because of the Court of Auditors' recommendations, but also because of developments in the field of patents, we urgently need to give this old-fashioned institution a new legal framework, that we take up mistakes from the past, but above all we also benefit future potential and future opportunities for the Republic. In the past, raw materials, transport costs, labour costs and energy costs were the crucial criteria. This has now been replaced by the knowledge factor – knowing how to provide services, how to produce products. And, of course, the question is also how to protect this knowledge in a global world. These are key challenges for the Austrian Patent Office. This is, of course, about information specifically for SMEs, namely not only the protection of intellectual property, but also, above all, how to deal with issues relating to the defence of patent infringements, how to deal with it, if one is inadvertently injured because one has not had the information. In the future, these are all tasks for the Austrian Patent Office, which we should stand by. ParlaMint-AT_2016-07-07-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00138_d7e22360	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! European patent laws prohibit the patenting of plants and animals, especially if they are derived from biological processes. As far as the jurisdictions are concerned. But then, by the European Patent Office, it came to patent broccoli and paradisiacs, although both were bred according to conventional, i.e. biological procedures. The excitement was great and the outrage in the population enormous, because no one understands it. As a result, an avalanche of applications has been made to the European Patent Office. Around 180 more patents have already been granted, and over 1,200 patents are, as it were, in the waiting loop for plants and animals to be patented. The citizen asks: How's that? How can this happen if Europe does not really want it? The fact is that the European Patent Office interprets the legal sciences in a particularly pointed way, namely by saying that traditional breeding methods are not patentable, but plants and animals that are bred with traditional breeding methods are very good. This is a contradiction that no one understands. It is also extremely incomprehensible. In my opinion, this practice of the European Patent Office is absolutely essential because it is against the spirit of European laws. Many people are disrupting the patenting of plants and animals. It interferes with the patenting of life, and I fully agree with that view. In Austria, Arche Noah, Bio Austria and PRO-GE have launched a petition against the patenting of plants and animals that have signed about 127 000 people. I have also signed and many thousands, hundreds of thousands of people in Europe, who are opposed to the patenting of life, animals, animal breeds and plant varieties. I have always been committed to the fact that we have a wide variety in preserving the variety of varieties of old, traditional farmed pet breeds and plant varieties. We'll need that even more in the future. At the time, we have been committed to the fact that the EU Seed Regulation, which was very much restricted here, did not come to give prospects to the small producers, but also to the local seed industry, which is a medium-sized one, and not to be dominated by large corporations. At that time we were successful, and so we are today – my colleague, Himmelbauer, I may mention, here, my littleness – in order not to patent plants and animals and not to obtain patents on life. Thank you very much to the operators of the petition and also to the many people who signed it. Thousands also passed on their concerns by e-mail. We can send a positive message to these people today, namely: We are innovation the Austrian Patent Act by making a clarification that not only is the patenting of plants and animals prohibited, but essentially of animals and plants derived from biological processes. We also see it as a strong signal to the European level, where it is necessary – and this is also the meaning of our resolution – that the Austrian Federal Government, both in Munich and in Brussels in particular, is working to ensure that the European materials, the EU Biopatent Directive, are clarified, so that it does not come to such a pointed interpretation that no human understands. It is important that Austria is committed to this because the European Commission plans to make such a clarification in autumn. But it is not yet clear in which direction. Germany, France, Switzerland, the Netherlands and Austria support the European Commission, which intends to interpret it in our sense. But the match is far from won. It is therefore important that Austria and also the Austrian Federal Government are very committed to this. The fact is, however, that breeding and research are developing further and this is a very complex matter in which grey zones float between biological methods and other methods. There are many new breeding methods. It is very important – and this has resulted in our discussion – that we deal with this matter in detail and in a technical way, because we have, for example, recognized research institutions such as the University of Soil Culture, as well as other private scientific research institutions in the field of medicine in Austria. It is important that we continue the discussion in autumn, and I can say in the final sentence: In times of climate change and global food security, it is important that small growers and medium-sized Austrian seed industry have free access to biological breeding material, because only this guarantees innovation and the development of new varieties that we urgently need. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-07-07-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00138_d7e23149	Thank you very much, Mr President. Federal Minister! Last year, in July 2015, the Constitutional Court found that the exemption from broadcasting charges was illegal. The aim of this amendment is to remove these found constitutional irregularities with amendments to the Radio Fees Act, the Telecommunications Fees Act, the Telecommunications Fees Regulation and the Telecoms Fee Grant Act and the Regulations to which reference is made. In the case of the constitutional irregularities, it has been noted that: In the view of the Constitutional Court of First Instance, there is an illegality in that the limitation of the deduction to rental conditions subject to the rental law, in a way contrary to Article 7 B-VG, leads to an imbalance in the treatment of leases under the rental law over leases outside the rental law, which was also subject by the legislator to an anti-mittent regime. In addition, the Constitutional Court of First Instance has also declared the previous consideration of the home rate by the GIS of 1 250 Schilling per month in 1986, last with 105,38 € not permissible, because the reference regulation has never been properly notified. What innovations does this novelle bring to the fore? In the future, there will be the deductibility of the main rental rate, including operating costs under the rental law, but also according to the housing public utility law and comparable Mieterschutzergesetzen. For the purposes of equal treatment, a flat rate of 140 € for all other forms of housing will be payable in the future. Another necessary adjustment is the change to the concept of net income. In this context, it is clear that the income of the caregiver who is living at the site of a person to be cared for cannot be taken into account from the income of other persons living in the household. And another innovation, which I would like to conclude, is in the 24 hour care. In the future, there will be the possibility of direct proof of the expenses incurred in the 24-hour care, for which a grant is also granted to support the 24-hour care provided by the Ministry of Social Affairs. In other words, this new scheme allows broadcasting operators to apply exceptional charges under Sections 34 and 35 of the Income Tax Act 1988 immediately. I think that this is an important innovation of the laws addressed, especially for those people who do not have it so easily anyway. And we will be very pleased to accept this bill and the amendment. Thank you.

(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) I repeat, but I think you can't say it often enough: We will never be able to win in international competition with the lowest wages, with the cheapest products, with the cheapest services, with the lowest social and environmental standards, but it can only work through the best minds, about the best ideas, about innovative products and services. We are therefore also in the Europe-wide comparison behind Finland and Sweden, even before Germany in third place of the most research-intensive countries. But it is also quite clear to say: On the way to the Innovation Leader, which is really the world’s top of the most research-intensive and strongest countries, there is still a lot to do. There are some areas where we have already gone a long way in the past – we have already discussed it in the framework of the research and technology report – but unfortunately we need a long breath in many areas, despite all impatience. There are still many areas in which we definitely need to get better – I take the Sigi Maurer straight ahead –: It is the field of basic research, our science and research in general also at universities, the education system, issues of risk capital, and questions of the position of research, technology and innovation in general in society. In the field of public research funding, our expenditure is quite high in international comparison, especially in the field of the promotion of applied research. However, the objective is always that every tax euro in the field of public research funding should develop as much as possible, so that the lever can also be used to increase the company's share of further euros from the corporate sector as well. Now, like any sophisticated economy, we have in reality a variety of instruments, and essentially these are two: One is direct research funding, in which we as politicians also propose concrete projects and priorities – BMVIT, for example, which now sets a very important focus with production technologies in the future, Industry 4.0. These are areas in which focus is conscious, as planning security is lower for companies, as this is very competitively awarded. In addition, there are other areas, which are equally necessary, which is the indirect research funding, the so-called research premium in Austria, in which 12 percent of the research performance of each company can also be applied tax-effectively. That has some advantages. It is lower, the planning security is higher. The question is: How does the control effect look, especially in the area of this lever effect? [[The President-in-Office of the Council shall take over the Presidency.]] We have now discussed this very question together in the Committee. I should like to thank all the political groups here in the House once again for taking this together. We also have to consider, qualitatively and quantitatively, how the research premium works. Can we improve something here, with the aim that we really use every tax euro optimally, so that we really are among the world's leaders in the field of innovation?

ParlaMint-AT_2016-07-07-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00138_d7e24225	We're coming to the 32nd. Agenda item. The first is Mr Hell. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2016-07-07-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00138_d7e24249	Mr President. Mrs. Volksanwaltin! Mr. Volksanwalt! We are now dealing with 39. Report of the Public Prosecutor's Office. The Public Prosecutor's Committee has discussed this report in two meetings. The report was again published in two volumes, the first of which deals with the control of public administration and the second with preventive human rights controls. This is a very detailed report, which impressively documents the activities of the Public Prosecutor's Office. It shows structural weaknesses and, in some individual cases, also gives insight into the situation as found by the public prosecutor's office. The Public Prosecutor's Office handled a total of 17,231 concerns and complaints in the public administration in 2015. In 8 181 cases – around 48 percent – the public prosecutor's office initiated a formal test procedure. Most testing procedures have been launched in the field of internal security. This trend was already apparent in the previous year, due to the large number of foreign and azylical complaints. Nearly the same number of examination procedures have been initiated in the social field, with problems in social security law or in the labour market. Ladies and gentlemen, access to the People's Procuratorate should be easy and easy for the people. As part of the speech, people in all the federal states have the opportunity to discuss their concerns personally with the People's Procuratorate – the Volksanwälte and the Volksanwälterin. In the year under review, 243 spokesmen with almost 1,500 personal talks. In addition, the complaints can be filed by telephone and in writing. A very simple complaint form can be found on the homepage, which is also very much used. It is striking that more men than women still turn to the People's Procuratorate. In 2015, an IMAS study was conducted showing that seven out of ten people surveyed know the public prosecutor's office and that citizens' proximity and commitment to the public are highly assessed. It is likely that the TV broadcast “Citizen’s Advocate” will also contribute significantly. The second part of the report highlights the human rights monitoring of the People's Procuratorate and its Commissions. Within the framework of the national prevention mechanism, the public prosecutors and their six commissions regularly control public and private bodies. Since July 2012, 1 575 Commission operations have been recorded. In 2015, 501 controls were carried out, 445 of which were carried out, focusing on classical places of residence such as judicial institutions, police inspections and police detention centres, as well as on old and nursing homes, psychiatry, hospitals and children's and youth institutions. In addition, 56 police missions were observed. My very much appreciated Ladies and gentlemen, the public prosecutorship as an institution is highly regarded in the population. Behind that are our lawyers with their employees. I should like to thank you very much, on behalf of the citizens, for their work.

The Public Prosecutor's Office has been home to the General Secretariat of the International Ombudsman Institute since 2009. This Institute is a non-political international organisation for independent administrative control bodies and combines around 170 independent Ombudsman institutions from more than 100 countries worldwide. There are a number of initiatives to promote and develop the concept of the Ombudsman's institution, such as training, seminars, training programmes or the exchange of information and experience on a case database for the Member States. The main focus of the work is on how the Institute can provide its members with the best possible support to enable them to exercise their role in the light of current challenges. The Public Prosecutor's Office also cooperates internationally with the EU institutions, the Council of Europe, the United Nations and the OSCE. Point Report of the Public Prosecutor's Committee on the Special Report of the Public Prosecutor's Office on the Perceptions of the Examining Procedure for the Anhaltezentrum Fürdenberg (III-188/1181 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2016-07-07-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00138_d7e25572	We're coming to 33. Agenda item. The first speech comes from Mr Hechtl. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_2016-07-07-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00138_d7e25597	As announced in the Special Report to the Anhaltezentrum Fürdenberg. The Public Prosecutor's Office has reviewed this secondary centre on the basis of assumptions, including the area in which it was concerned whether the performance of certain outsourced activities is actually not a sovereign act. The Ministry of the Interior has jurisdiction. The Ministry of the Interior has concluded a contract for the activities with the municipality of Vorsenberg, and the municipality of Vorsenberg has in turn concluded a contract with a private security firm that carries out activities for it. The People's Procuratorate has examined this. The presumption that this security firm has done sovereign tasks has hardened itself. As a result, the contract was amended. This is a success of the People's Procuratorate. We, ladies and gentlemen, do not share human rights and have no borders either. I would like to congratulate the public prosecutor on this special report, which is really very detailed. However, I would also like to point out that the Commission, which has examined this centre four times, has clearly stated that medical care there, documentation, psychosocial care, hygiene and food standards can be considered as good. I would like to thank the People's Procuratorate once again for this detailed report.
ParlaMint-AT_2016-07-07-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00138_d7e25636	The next speaker is Mrs Fekter. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2016-07-07-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00138_d7e25657	Mr President. Dear colleague Volksanwälter and dear colleagues Volksanwälte! – I can say this: On the one hand, you have been Members of Parliament for a long time, on the other hand, I was a popular lawyer. I thank you for this comprehensive special report on the Anhaltezentrum Fürderenberg. Vorderenberg is a project from my time at the Ministry of Interior. I am very pleased that the Commissions who have visited this House are very positive about this and that the Special Report contains very positive documentation as a whole, even though it has only occasionally been created by the elimination of childhood diseases. The involvement of private security forces is generally seen as positive by the People's Procuratorate, but it suggests that we should make our own security law in this regard. I welcome it. We know this from the airport, from there security services – even there we have our own law – and I also know it from the judicial agency, where the judicial service also has its own law with regard to the different use of private persons. That means that this is an assignment to the legislator, and we will see that we will implement it as soon as possible. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-07-07-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00138_d7e25709	Mr President. Dear Mrs. Volksanstalt! Mr. Volksanwälte! The best always comes last. This is a saying which applies to this agenda in two ways: firstly, because this Anhaltezentrum Fürdenberg is in reality a parade example of maladministration, legal insignificance and wastefulness – I am going to explain it to you, Mrs Colleague! – and, on the other hand, because this particular item would have been part of the beginning of today’s agenda. [[Abg. Fekter: What report do you have? I have no such thing!]] Then, too, the observers on ORF 2 and other people might have been able to see what misunderstandings actually exist. In fact, only a cabaretist mission by Reinhard Nowak, an Austrian cabaretist, was needed to look at Pulse 4, then, like him, one would conclude: “Are you depressing!” From the beginning, from the tendering phase to the operation, so many peculiarities – I want to call them that way – are already peculiar. All this is strange because, on the occasion of the award of one of the largest service contracts for the Republic of Austria, a company, namely the VSÖ – the association of the security companies of Austria, which consists of a community of four other major security companies, including the G4S – has issued a manual before the start of the call for tenders, which is one thing. On the other hand, our previous Minister of the Interior Mikl- Leitner writes the preface for this booklet. - Strange! Then the municipality of Vorderenberg writes this contract throughout Europe, and merely by chance a Vienna company is commissioned, which uses this very manual as the basis for the tendering procedure. – Again strange! Then you look a little further: G4S is the sole provider of the tender. You might ask yourself: Yes, how is that possible? – And I realize that Matthias Wechner, then managing director of the then Ministry of the Interior, was the deputy managing director of Platter. – Strange and pure coincidence! Former Minister Ernst Strasser was in the Supervisory Board of the company G4S. – Also strange and all the purest coincidence! Then the biggest thing at all is: The offer had to be below a maximum offer, which of course was strictly secret – Mrs. Colleague? After that it was like this: The municipality of Vorsenberg receives €9 799 for snow clearing. That's a proud amount, but it's supposed to be. If you deduct this amount from the maximum amount, the company G4S offers an offer which is exactly 99 percent of this tender offer. - Strange again! She did one thing: It has found out how high the cost of snow clearing is, and at the same time how high the maximum offer is. [[Abg. Matznetter: The Meischberger doesn't happen at all?- Oh, my God!]] - You don't need a meschberger, you've done that. [[Intervocations with SPÖ and Greens.]] – We can then continue to discuss. But now it comes first: After that, you conclude a contract for an indefinite period of time, which can only be terminated after 16 years! - Well, it's not about anything. It is actually only about €446,988 in costs per month, 16 years. That's 5 €363,856 a year and in 16 years, if we can cancel it, it's €85 million and a few squeaks, regardless of whether or not this secondary center might be used in a few years or not. That's a strong piece! – Yes, for what have you designed that? – For 200 people or stopped, and ten people you have had most of the time inside, and the costs have been paid by the National Police Directorate of Styria. [[Abg. Fekter: But for that we were already!]] It may have been able to beg her customers because she could not pay the bills at times. - Great conditions, so really great! - No! [[Call: They could have distributed more prisons]] But let's go on. That is why I am so grateful to the People's Procuratorate that she really took up this issue and also looked at whether it might at least fit in constitutional terms. But even there it didn't fit. The colleague said: The contract was flicked, had to be improved, because it was unconstitutional. But it was a good throw by directing private companies to take care of employees, and at the same time do not even know what responsibilities are conferred on them. Where is the demarcation, where is the competence? – A great performance, I have to say! If you say today – and it's already right – the prisoners are well cared for, then I have to say, that's all clear on these conditions. But basically, I can only say one thing: “Are you down!” – Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-07-07-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00138_d7e25813	Mr President. Estimated Mrs. Volksanwaltin! Mr. Volksanwälte! Dear Ladies and Gentlemen! I think it is generally known that I rarely agree with the speeches of colleagues who are free to speak. In this case, I really have to make an exception because unfortunately, one can only join this analysis of the unsustainable states. In this sense: Thank you to the previous speaker for formulating it so openly! I would like to start with a thank you to the People's Procuratorate. This report on the Schubhaftzentrum Fürdenberg is the first special report of this kind. I think it is good, but also significant for the conditions that exist and are also presented and analysed in the report, that the Public Prosecutor's Office has just decided to look more closely at these conditions, from a legal and constitutional point of view. I think it is a strong piece, my dear Mrs Fekter, if you make this work, so to speak, so to speak, as light-footed as these gag contracts that originate from your time: Well, and now we might need to change a law, because the People's Procuratorate has pointed this out. The special report of the Public Prosecutor's Office confirms all our criticisms from the very beginning that this partial privatisation of this detention prison also partially privatized sovereign tasks. I would like to quote briefly from this report. – The People’s Procuratorate argues that it has carried out this special examination on the basis of the presumption that sovereign tasks have been out to private security forces. I quote from the report: “This presumption” – namely that constitutional tasks are unlawfully outsourced to private security forces – “has been confirmed in the course of the examination process, in particular with regard to the powers to ‘enforce’ the house rules in the secondary centre, as well as in the area of violence and conflict prevention, and so on. The Public Prosecutor's Office also says in its report – I quote again –: ‘Constitutional highly problematic appears to be the ‘ factual exclusion’ of sovereignty powers. This is especially because the protection of inmates against assaults," - Caution, human rights! – “which would primarily be achieved by the use of state-of-the-art (...) executives to replace de facto by the exercise of everyone’s rights (...) by private security forces.” The People's Procuratorate continues: “Even this lack could” – could! – “by constant presence of executives in the housing areas of the AHZ, however, this has been rejected by the BMI until the last.” – Status: May 2015. These conditions, where constitutionally state-of-the-art tasks have been privatized, where a dubious tender procedure has ensured that one of the largest private security companies, namely G4S, is awarded the award, where, as the colleague of the liberals rightly mentioned earlier, the former Vice-Chairman of an ÖVP Minister of the Interior was and is, by chance, the managing director of the former Vice-Chairman of the ÖVP, where, by chance, a gag contract is signed, which can only be dissolved after 16 years, where almost half a million euros of tax money is paid per month, even if there are two inmates there, which has also happened at times – this is, unfortunately, a declaration of bankruptcy, which has taken place under an EIA-led internal ministry – where you really have to say the Chuzpe, to sit down and say: Yes, the People's Procurator. We have to make a law to reorganize the whole thing. The Unfortunately, it smells like heaven, and you have to say: If one knows these conditions, then one understands what the ÖVP clearly means by “More private, less state”, namely privatisation of sovereign tasks, granting contracts to private security companies, where former ÖVP cabinet workers are sitting by chance, with a gag contract where we are allowed to pay about half a million euros for 16 years as citizens of our tax contributions month after month. Thank you to the People's Procuratorate that these states are now black on white! The consequence may and must be that these contracts are dissolved. In this sense, I hope for cooperation that we will finally end up or work on these agreements together. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Fekter.]] – Thank you for the attention.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-07-07-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00138_d7e25901	Mr President. Mrs. Volksanwaltin! Dear People's Lawyers! Thank you very much for the report. I think what you have written here is particularly important. It is quite historical: If, on a human rights issue, Mr Korun, Mr Kumpitsch and I agree, it can only be a matter of one thing, namely the legacy of colleague Fekter in the Ministry of the Interior: high-problem alerts, and in such a fundamental-right-sensitive area! [[Congratulation with NEOS, SPÖ and Greens.]] We've heard the problem, it's about: What happens when private security service providers, who can be entrusted with certain tasks without further delay, are commissioned in the area of fundamental rights-sensitive? – If fundamental rights violations occur and happen here, then there is no way of dealing with them. If the police do something and an infringement of fundamental rights happens, then there is a handling, namely the action complaint. There's no such thing here. There is a legal gap that needs to be closed, which we as a Parliament must close and which we could have closed for a long time, above all. Because when we thought, I think, almost a year and a half, when the issue came up for the first time, that discussion had been held in the Human Rights Committee, we have in turn said unisono liberals, Greens and NEOS: We have a legal gap and have to do something. Mrs Fekter is daily eating and said: It's not a problem, the State Police Directorate Steiermark told me it's all right, we don't have a legal gap here. Mrs Fekter! This is the great example of how good it would be if you were to listen to the opposition. There are three opposition parties here who are very far apart in the area of human rights and in themselves, quite clearly, what to do. We could have done it. It has not happened, but it is most embarrassing that we as Parliament, as legislators, have not done so. In particular, we must take care of fundamental rights that we could have closed a legal gap here. It's good you've seen it now. Thank you again to the Volksanwälte for this report! We have to make it faster, as fast as possible, because what can't be, It is that we undermine the state's monopoly of violence. We know what this led to: You only need to look into the United States to see what this led to. I consider these activities to be a prime task of the state. If we avoid the state's monopoly of violence, we have a massive problem on a sustainable basis. [[President Head gives the bell sign]]
